					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Head of the Catholic Church from 1903 to 1914</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">This article is about the pope who was canonized. For the namesake fraternity, see <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Saint_Pius_X" title="Society of Saint Pius X">Society of Saint Pius X</a>. For other uses, see <a href="/wiki/St._Pius_X_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="St. Pius X (disambiguation)">St. Pius X (disambiguation)</a>.</div>

font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacques-Jean_Gely" class="extiw" title="fr:Jacques-Jean Gely">fr</a>&#93;</span></th><td class="infobox-data">25 February 1906</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Giacomo_Giambattista_della_Chiesa" class="mw-redirect" title="Giacomo Giambattista della Chiesa">Giacomo Giambattista della Chiesa</a></th><td class="infobox-data">22 December 1907</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Gaetano_Cardinal_De_Lai" class="mw-redirect" title="Gaetano Cardinal De Lai">Gaetano Cardinal De Lai</a></th><td class="infobox-data">17 December 1911</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Adam_Stefan_Cardinal_Sapieha" class="mw-redirect" title="Adam Stefan Cardinal Sapieha">Adam Stefan Cardinal Sapieha</a></th><td class="infobox-data">17 December 1911</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pie_Armand_Pierre_Sabadel&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Pie Armand Pierre Sabadel (page does not exist)">Pie Armand Pierre Sabadel</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pie_Armand_Pierre_Sabadel" class="extiw" title="fr:Pie Armand Pierre Sabadel">fr</a>&#93;</span></th><td class="infobox-data">17 December 1911</td></tr></tbody></table></td></tr><tr style="display:none"><td colspan="2">
  </td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below"><a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_(disambiguation)" class="mw-redirect mw-disambig" title="Pope Pius (disambiguation)">Other popes  named Pius</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Pope Pius X</b> (<a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Pio X</i>; born <b>Giuseppe Melchiorre Sarto</b>;<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> 2 June 1835&#160;– 20 August 1914) was head of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> from 4 August 1903 to his death in August 1914. Pius X is known for vigorously opposing <a href="/wiki/Modernism_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Modernism in the Catholic Church">modernist</a> interpretations of <a href="/wiki/Ten_Commandments_in_Catholic_theology" title="Ten Commandments in Catholic theology">Catholic doctrine</a>, and for promoting liturgical reforms and scholastic theology. He initiated the preparation of the <a href="/wiki/1917_Code_of_Canon_Law" title="1917 Code of Canon Law">1917 Code of Canon Law</a>, the first comprehensive and systemic work of its kind. He is venerated as a <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">saint</a> in the Catholic Church. The <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Saint_Pius_X" title="Society of Saint Pius X">Society of Saint Pius X</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Traditionalist_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Traditionalist Catholic">traditionalist Catholic</a> fraternity, is named after him.
</p><p>Pius X was devoted to the <a href="/wiki/Blessed_Virgin_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Blessed Virgin Mary">Blessed Virgin Mary</a> under the <a href="/wiki/Marian_title" class="mw-redirect" title="Marian title">title</a> of <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Confidence" title="Our Lady of Confidence">Our Lady of Confidence</a>; while his papal <a href="/wiki/Encyclical" title="Encyclical">encyclical</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Ad_diem_illum" title="Ad diem illum">Ad diem illum</a></i> took on a sense of renewal that was reflected in the motto of his pontificate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESarto19045_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESarto19045-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> He advanced the <a href="/wiki/Liturgical_Movement" title="Liturgical Movement">Liturgical Movement</a> by formulating the principle of <i>participatio actuosa</i> (active participation of the faithful) in his <a href="/wiki/Motu_proprio" title="Motu proprio">motu proprio</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Tra_le_sollecitudini" class="mw-redirect" title="Tra le sollecitudini">Tra le sollecitudini</a></i> (1903). He encouraged the frequent reception of <a href="/wiki/Holy_Communion" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Communion">Holy Communion</a>, and he lowered the age for <a href="/wiki/First_Communion" title="First Communion">First Communion</a>, which became a lasting innovation of his papacy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELortz1934§113_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELortz1934§113-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Like his predecessors, he promoted <a href="/wiki/Thomism" title="Thomism">Thomism</a> as the principal philosophical method to be taught in Catholic institutions. He vehemently opposed various 19th-century philosophies that he viewed as an intrusion of secular errors incompatible with <a href="/wiki/Catholic_dogma" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic dogma">Catholic dogma</a>, especially modernism, which he critiqued as the synthesis of every <a href="/wiki/Heresy" title="Heresy">heresy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELortz1934§113,2_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELortz1934§113,2-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius X was known for his firm demeanour and sense of personal poverty, reflected by his membership of the <a href="/wiki/Third_Order_of_Saint_Francis" title="Third Order of Saint Francis">Third Order of Saint Francis</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKühner1960183_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKühner1960183-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> He regularly gave <a href="/wiki/Sermon" title="Sermon">sermons</a> from the <a href="/wiki/Pulpit" title="Pulpit">pulpit</a>, a rare practice at the time.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup> After the <a href="/wiki/1908_Messina_earthquake" title="1908 Messina earthquake">1908 Messina earthquake</a> he filled the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Palace" title="Apostolic Palace">Apostolic Palace</a> with refugees, long before the Italian government acted.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKühner1960183_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKühner1960183-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> He rejected any kind of favours for his family, and his close relatives chose to remain in poverty, living near Rome.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKühner1960183_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKühner1960183-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-franciscan-sfo.org_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-franciscan-sfo.org-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> He also undertook a reform of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a> with the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Constitution" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Constitution">Apostolic Constitution</a> <i>Sapienti consilio</i> in 1908.
</p><p>After his death, a strong cult of devotion followed his reputation for piety and holiness. He was <a href="/wiki/Beatified" class="mw-redirect" title="Beatified">beatified</a> in 1951 and <a href="/wiki/Canonized" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonized">canonized</a> in 1954 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-franciscan-sfo.org_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-franciscan-sfo.org-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> A statue bearing his name stands within <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Basilica">Saint Peter's Basilica</a>, and his birth town was renamed <a href="/wiki/Riese_Pio_X" title="Riese Pio X">Riese Pio X</a> after his death.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life_and_ministry">Early life and ministry</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life and ministry">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Our_Lady_of_Confidence.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/32/Our_Lady_of_Confidence.jpg" decoding="async" width="214" height="260" class="mw-file-element" data-file-width="214" data-file-height="260" /></a><figcaption>The Marian image of <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Confidence" title="Our Lady of Confidence">Our Lady of Confidence</a>, for whom Pius X had a religious devotion. The <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Saint_John_Lateran" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica of Saint John Lateran">Basilica of Saint John Lateran</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Giuseppe Melchiorre Sarto was born in <a href="/wiki/Riese_Pio_X" title="Riese Pio X">Riese</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Lombardy%E2%80%93Venetia" title="Kingdom of Lombardy–Venetia">Kingdom of Lombardy–Venetia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Empire" title="Austrian Empire">Austrian Empire</a> (now in the <a href="/wiki/Province_of_Treviso" title="Province of Treviso">province of Treviso</a>, <a href="/wiki/Veneto" title="Veneto">Veneto</a>, <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>), in 1835. He was the second born of ten children of Giovanni Battista Sarto (1792–1852), the village <a href="/wiki/Postman" class="mw-redirect" title="Postman">postman</a>, and Margherita Sanson (1813–1894). He was baptised 3 June 1835. Though poor, his parents valued education, and Giuseppe walked 6 kilometres (3.7&#160;mi) to school each day.
</p><p>Giuseppe had three brothers and six sisters: Giuseppe Sarto (born 1834; died after six days), Angelo Sarto (1837–1916), Teresa Parolin-Sarto (1839–1920), Rosa Sarto (1841–1913), Antonia Dei Bei-Sarto (1843–1917), Maria Sarto (1846–1930), Lucia Boschin-Sarto (1848–1924), Anna Sarto (1850–1926), Pietro Sarto (born 1852; died after six months).<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> As Pope, he rejected any kind of favours for his family: his brother remained a postal clerk, his favourite nephew stayed on as village priest, and his three single sisters lived together close to poverty in Rome, in the same way as other people of humble background.
</p><p>Giuseppe, often nicknamed as "Bepi" by his mother, possessed a sprightly disposition with his natural exuberance being so great that his teacher had to often control his lively impulses with a cane to the backside. Despite this, he was an excellent student who focused on his homework before engaging in any hobbies or recreations. In the evenings after sports or games with friends, he would spend ten minutes in prayer before returning home.<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Sarto also served as an altar boy. By the age of ten, he had completed the two elementary classes of his village school, as well as Latin study with a local priest; henceforth Sarto had to walk four miles to the <a href="/wiki/Gymnasium_(school)" title="Gymnasium (school)">gymnasium</a> in <a href="/wiki/Castelfranco_Veneto" title="Castelfranco Veneto">Castelfranco Veneto</a> for further classes. For the next four years, he would attend Mass before breakfast and his long walk to school. He often carried his shoes to make them last longer. As a poor boy, he was often teased for his meager lunches and shabby clothes, but never complained about this to his teachers.<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1850 he received the <a href="/wiki/Tonsure" title="Tonsure">tonsure</a> from his parish priest, and was given a scholarship [from] the Cardinal of Venice (who his parish priest had written, asking for a scholarship for Bepi) to attend the Seminary of <a href="/wiki/Padua" title="Padua">Padua</a>, "where he finished his classical, philosophical, and theological studies with distinction".<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mlad%C3%BD_Jozef_Sarto_-_neskor%C5%A1%C3%AD_p%C3%A1pe%C5%BE_Pius_X..jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Mlad%C3%BD_Jozef_Sarto_-_neskor%C5%A1%C3%AD_p%C3%A1pe%C5%BE_Pius_X..jpg/220px-Mlad%C3%BD_Jozef_Sarto_-_neskor%C5%A1%C3%AD_p%C3%A1pe%C5%BE_Pius_X..jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="303" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/40/Mlad%C3%BD_Jozef_Sarto_-_neskor%C5%A1%C3%AD_p%C3%A1pe%C5%BE_Pius_X..jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="290" data-file-height="400" /></a><figcaption>A young Giuseppe Sarto</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 18 September 1858, Sarto was ordained a priest by <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Antonio_Farina" title="Giovanni Antonio Farina">Giovanni Antonio Farina</a> (later canonized), and became a chaplain at <a href="/wiki/Tombolo,_Veneto" title="Tombolo, Veneto">Tombolo</a>. While there, Sarto expanded his knowledge of theology, studying both <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Aquinas" title="Thomas Aquinas">Thomas Aquinas</a> and <a href="/wiki/Canon_law_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon law (Catholic Church)">canon law</a>, while carrying out most of the functions of the parish pastor Constantini, who was quite ill. Often, Sarto sought to improve his sermons by the advice of Constantini, who referred to one of his earliest as "rubbish".<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> In Tombolo, Sarto's reputation for holiness grew so much amongst the people that some suggested the nickname "Don Santo".<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1867, he was named <a href="/wiki/Archpriest" title="Archpriest">archpriest</a> of <a href="/wiki/Salzano" title="Salzano">Salzano</a>. Here he restored the church and expanded the hospital, the funds coming from his own begging, wealth and labour. He won the people's affection when he worked to assist the sick during the <a href="/wiki/Cholera" title="Cholera">cholera</a> plague of the early 1870s. He was named a <a href="/wiki/Canon_(priest)" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon (priest)">canon</a> of the cathedral and <a href="/wiki/Chancellor" title="Chancellor">chancellor</a> of the Diocese of Treviso, also holding offices such as spiritual director and <a href="/wiki/Rector_(ecclesiastical)" title="Rector (ecclesiastical)">rector</a> of the Treviso seminary, and examiner of the clergy. As chancellor he made it possible for <a href="/wiki/Public_school_(government_funded)" class="mw-redirect" title="Public school (government funded)">public school</a> students to receive religious instruction. As a priest and later bishop, he often struggled over solving problems of bringing religious instruction to rural and urban youth who did not have the opportunity to attend Catholic schools. At one stage, a large stack of hay caught fire near a cottage, and when Sarto arrived he addressed the frantic people, "Don't be afraid, the fire will be put out and your house will be saved!" At that moment, the flames turned in the other direction, leaving the cottage alone.<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Despite his many duties, Sarto often made time for an evening walk with young children preparing their <a href="/wiki/First_Communion" title="First Communion">First Communion</a>.
</p><p>In 1879, <a href="/wiki/Federico_Maria_Zinelli" title="Federico Maria Zinelli">Bishop Federico Maria Zinelli</a> died,<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> and Sarto was elected vicar-capitular to care for the diocese until the accession of a new bishop in June 1880.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Biskup_Sarto.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Biskup_Sarto.jpg/220px-Biskup_Sarto.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="301" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/cf/Biskup_Sarto.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="292" data-file-height="400" /></a><figcaption>Bishop Giuseppe Sarto</figcaption></figure>
<p>After 1880, Sarto taught <a href="/wiki/Dogmatic_theology" title="Dogmatic theology">dogmatic theology</a> and <a href="/wiki/Moral_theology" class="mw-redirect" title="Moral theology">moral theology</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Seminary" title="Seminary">seminary</a> in Treviso. On 10 November 1884, he was appointed <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Mantua" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Mantua">bishop of Mantua</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a>. He was consecrated six days later in Rome in the church of <a href="/wiki/Sant%27Apollinare_alle_Terme_Neroniane-Alessandrine" class="mw-redirect" title="Sant&#39;Apollinare alle Terme Neroniane-Alessandrine">Sant'Apollinare alle Terme Neroniane-Alessandrine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>, by Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Lucido_Parocchi" title="Lucido Parocchi">Lucido Parocchi</a>, assisted by <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Rota" title="Pietro Rota">Pietro Rota</a>, and by Giovanni Maria Berengo. He was appointed to the honorary position of <a href="/wiki/Assistant_at_the_pontifical_throne" class="mw-redirect" title="Assistant at the pontifical throne">assistant at the pontifical throne</a> on 19 June 1891. Sarto required papal dispensation from Pope Leo XIII before episcopal consecration as he lacked a doctorate,<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> making him the last pope without a doctorate until <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a>.
</p><p>When Sarto travelled back to his hometown from Rome after his consecration, he immediately went to visit his mother. There, she repeatedly kissed his ring and said to him: "But you would not have this fine ring, son, if I did not have this", showing him her <a href="/wiki/Wedding_ring" title="Wedding ring">wedding ring</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cardinalate_and_patriarchate">Cardinalate and patriarchate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Cardinalate and patriarchate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kardin%C3%A1l_Sarto.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Kardin%C3%A1l_Sarto.jpg/220px-Kardin%C3%A1l_Sarto.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="296" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/f9/Kardin%C3%A1l_Sarto.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="297" data-file-height="400" /></a><figcaption>Photo as Cardinal Giuseppe Sarto</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a> made him a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">cardinal</a> of the order of cardinal priests in a secret <a href="/wiki/Papal_consistory" title="Papal consistory">consistory</a> on 12 June 1893.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Three days later in a public consistory on 15 June, Pope Leo XIII gave him his cardinal's red <a href="/wiki/Galero" title="Galero">galero</a>, assigned him the titular church of <a href="/wiki/San_Bernardo_alle_Terme" title="San Bernardo alle Terme">San Bernardo alle Terme</a>, and appointed him <a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_Venice" title="Patriarch of Venice">Patriarch of Venice</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> This caused difficulty, however, as the government of the reunified Italy claimed the right to nominate the Patriarch, since the previous sovereign, the <a href="/wiki/Emperor_of_Austria" title="Emperor of Austria">Emperor of Austria</a>, had exercised that power. The poor relations between the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a> and the Italian civil government since the <a href="/wiki/Capture_of_Rome" title="Capture of Rome">annexation of the Papal States</a> in 1870 placed additional strain on the appointment. The number of vacant <a href="/wiki/Episcopal_See" class="mw-redirect" title="Episcopal See">sees</a> soon grew to 30. Sarto was finally permitted to assume the position of patriarch in 1894. In regard to being named as a cardinal, Sarto told a local newspaper that he felt "anxious, terrified and humiliated".<sup id="cite_ref-SPC_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPC-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After being named cardinal and before leaving for Venice, he paid his mother a visit. Overwhelmed with emotion and in tears, she asked: "My son, give your mother a last blessing", sensing that it would be the last time that they would see each other.<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Arriving in Venice, he was formally enthroned on 24 November 1894.
</p><p>As cardinal-patriarch, Sarto avoided politics, allocating his time to social works and strengthening parochial credit unions. However, in his first <a href="/wiki/Pastoral_letter" title="Pastoral letter">pastoral letter</a> to the Venetians, Sarto argued that in matters pertaining to the pope, "There should be no questions, no subtleties, no opposing of personal rights to his rights, but only obedience."<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In April 1903, Pope Leo XIII reportedly said to <a href="/wiki/Lorenzo_Perosi" title="Lorenzo Perosi">Lorenzo Perosi</a>: "Hold him very dear, Perosi, as in the future he will be able to do much for you. We firmly believe he will be our successor". As a cardinal, he was considered by the time of his papal election as one of the most prominent preachers in the Church despite his lesser fame globally.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> In his role as a cardinal, Sarto held membership in the congregations for <a href="/wiki/Congregation_of_Bishops_and_Regulars" title="Congregation of Bishops and Regulars">Bishops and Regulars</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sacred_Congregation_of_Rites" title="Sacred Congregation of Rites">Rites</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_Indulgences_and_Sacred_Relics" title="Congregation for Indulgences and Sacred Relics">Indulgences and Sacred Relics</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Papal_election_of_1903">Papal election of 1903</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Papal election of 1903">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1903_papal_conclave" title="1903 papal conclave">1903 papal conclave</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:HabemuspapmLuigiMacchi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/HabemuspapmLuigiMacchi.jpg/220px-HabemuspapmLuigiMacchi.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="159" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/HabemuspapmLuigiMacchi.jpg/330px-HabemuspapmLuigiMacchi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/HabemuspapmLuigiMacchi.jpg/440px-HabemuspapmLuigiMacchi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2832" data-file-height="2049" /></a><figcaption>Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Macchi" title="Luigi Macchi">Luigi Macchi</a> <a href="/wiki/Habemus_Papam" class="mw-redirect" title="Habemus Papam">announces the election</a> of Sarto as Pope Pius X.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Leo XIII died 20 July 1903, and at the end of that month the <a href="/wiki/Papal_Election" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal Election">conclave</a> convened to elect his successor. Before the conclave, Sarto had reportedly said, "rather dead than pope", when people discussed his chances for election. In one of the meetings held just before the conclave, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Victor-Lucien-Sulpice_L%C3%A9cot" title="Victor-Lucien-Sulpice Lécot">Victor-Lucien-Sulpice Lécot</a> spoke with Sarto in French, however, Sarto replied in Latin, "I'm afraid I do not speak French". Lécot replied, "But if Your Eminence does not speak French you have no chance of being elected because the pope must speak French", to which Sarto said, "Deo Gratias! I have no desire to be pope".<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to historians, the favorite was the late pope's secretary of state, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Mariano_Rampolla" title="Mariano Rampolla">Mariano Rampolla</a>. On the first ballot, Rampolla received 24 votes, Gotti had 17 votes, and Sarto 5 votes. On the second ballot, Rampolla had gained five votes, as did Sarto. The next day, it seemed that Rampolla would be elected. However, the Polish Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Jan_Puzyna_de_Kosielsko" title="Jan Puzyna de Kosielsko">Jan Puzyna de Kosielsko</a> from <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Krak%C3%B3w" title="Archbishop of Kraków">Kraków</a>, in the name of Emperor <a href="/wiki/Franz_Josef_I_of_Austria" class="mw-redirect" title="Franz Josef I of Austria">Franz Joseph</a> (1848–1916) of <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a>, proclaimed a veto (<i><a href="/wiki/Jus_exclusivae" title="Jus exclusivae">jus exclusivae</a></i>) against Rampolla's election.<sup id="cite_ref-Bingham_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bingham-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Many in the conclave protested, and it was even suggested to disregard the veto.
</p><p>However, the third vote had already begun, resulting in no clear winner but increasing support for Sarto, with 21 votes. The fourth vote showed Rampolla with 30 votes and Sarto with 24. It seemed clear that the cardinals were moving toward Sarto.
</p><p>The following morning, the fifth vote gave Rampolla 10 votes, Gotti 2, and Sarto 50.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlinde_Waal1904186ff_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlinde_Waal1904186ff-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Thus, on 4 August 1903, Sarto was elected to the pontificate. This marked the last known exercise of a papal veto by a Catholic monarch.
</p><p>At first, it is reported, Sarto declined the nomination, feeling unworthy. He had been deeply saddened by the Austro-Hungarian veto and vowed to rescind these powers and <a href="/wiki/Excommunication" title="Excommunication">excommunicate</a> anyone who communicated such a veto during a conclave.<sup id="cite_ref-Bingham_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bingham-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> With the cardinals asking him to reconsider, he went into solitude in the Pauline chapel, and after deep prayer he accepted the position. Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Macchi" title="Luigi Macchi">Luigi Macchi</a> announced Sarto's election at around 12:10pm.
</p><p>Sarto took as his papal name Pius X, out of respect for his recent predecessors of the same name, particularly <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX" title="Pope Pius IX">Pope Pius IX</a> (1846–1878), who had fought against theological liberals and for papal supremacy. He explained: "As I shall suffer, I shall take the name of those Popes who also suffered".<sup id="cite_ref-SPC_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPC-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Pius X's traditional <a href="/wiki/Papal_coronation" title="Papal coronation">coronation</a> took place the following Sunday, 9 August 1903. As pope, he became ex officio <a href="/wiki/Grand_Masters_and_Lieutenancies_of_the_Order_of_the_Holy_Sepulchre" title="Grand Masters and Lieutenancies of the Order of the Holy Sepulchre">Grand Master</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Equestrian_Order_of_the_Holy_Sepulchre_of_Jerusalem" class="mw-redirect" title="Equestrian Order of the Holy Sepulchre of Jerusalem">Equestrian Order of the Holy Sepulchre of Jerusalem</a>, <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith">prefect of the Supreme Sacred Congregation of the Holy Office</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_Bishops" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for Bishops">prefect of the Sacred Consistorial Congregation</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Pontificate">Pontificate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Pontificate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Spiox.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Spiox.jpg/220px-Spiox.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="308" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/8a/Spiox.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="247" data-file-height="346" /></a><figcaption>An official photograph of Pius X wearing <a href="/wiki/Papal_regalia" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal regalia">Papal regalia</a> on 14 August 1903</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: gold;"><span style="font-size:80%">Papal styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Pope Pius X</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:C_o_a_Pius_X.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/C_o_a_Pius_X.svg/60px-C_o_a_Pius_X.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="88" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/C_o_a_Pius_X.svg/90px-C_o_a_Pius_X.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/C_o_a_Pius_X.svg/120px-C_o_a_Pius_X.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="889" data-file-height="1297" /></a></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Holiness</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religious style</th><td class="infobox-data">Holy Father</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Posthumous style</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">Saint</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:PiusXvatgarden.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/PiusXvatgarden.jpg/220px-PiusXvatgarden.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="209" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/PiusXvatgarden.jpg/330px-PiusXvatgarden.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/PiusXvatgarden.jpg/440px-PiusXvatgarden.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1232" data-file-height="1170" /></a><figcaption>Pope Pius X resting in the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Gardens" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican Gardens">Vatican Gardens</a></figcaption></figure><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Color_Photograph_of_Pope_St._Pius_X,_NPG,_1907,_Series_2_No._1._NPG_(Neue_Photographische_Gesellschaft)_of_Steglitz_in_Berlin,_Germany.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Color_Photograph_of_Pope_St._Pius_X%2C_NPG%2C_1907%2C_Series_2_No._1._NPG_%28Neue_Photographische_Gesellschaft%29_of_Steglitz_in_Berlin%2C_Germany.png/220px-Color_Photograph_of_Pope_St._Pius_X%2C_NPG%2C_1907%2C_Series_2_No._1._NPG_%28Neue_Photographische_Gesellschaft%29_of_Steglitz_in_Berlin%2C_Germany.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="350" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Color_Photograph_of_Pope_St._Pius_X%2C_NPG%2C_1907%2C_Series_2_No._1._NPG_%28Neue_Photographische_Gesellschaft%29_of_Steglitz_in_Berlin%2C_Germany.png/330px-Color_Photograph_of_Pope_St._Pius_X%2C_NPG%2C_1907%2C_Series_2_No._1._NPG_%28Neue_Photographische_Gesellschaft%29_of_Steglitz_in_Berlin%2C_Germany.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Color_Photograph_of_Pope_St._Pius_X%2C_NPG%2C_1907%2C_Series_2_No._1._NPG_%28Neue_Photographische_Gesellschaft%29_of_Steglitz_in_Berlin%2C_Germany.png/440px-Color_Photograph_of_Pope_St._Pius_X%2C_NPG%2C_1907%2C_Series_2_No._1._NPG_%28Neue_Photographische_Gesellschaft%29_of_Steglitz_in_Berlin%2C_Germany.png 2x" data-file-width="2045" data-file-height="3250" /></a><figcaption>Pope Pius X in an early <a href="/wiki/Color_photography" title="Color photography">color photograph</a>, <span title="circa">c.</span><span style="white-space:nowrap;">&#8201;1907</span></figcaption></figure><p>The pontificate of Pius X was noted for conservative theology and reforms in liturgy and Church law. In what became his motto, the Pope in 1903 devoted his papacy to <i>Instaurare Omnia in Christo</i>, "to restore all things in Christ." In his first encyclical (<i><a href="/wiki/E_supremi_apostolatus" class="mw-redirect" title="E supremi apostolatus">E supremi apostolatus</a></i>, 4 October 1903), he stated his overriding policy: "We champion the authority of God. His authority and Commandments should be recognized, deferred to, and respected."
</p><p>Continuing his simple origins, he wore a pectoral cross of gilded metal on the day of his coronation; and when his entourage was horrified, the new pope declared he always wore it and had brought no other with him.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> He was well known for reducing papal ceremonies. He also abolished the custom of the pope dining alone, since the time of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Urban_VIII" title="Pope Urban VIII">Pope Urban VIII</a>, and invited his friends to eat with him.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When chided by Rome's social leaders for refusing to make his peasant sisters papal countesses, he responded: "I have made them sisters of the Pope; what more can I do for them?"<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He developed a reputation as being very friendly with children. He carried candy in his pockets for the street urchins in Mantua and Venice, and taught them <a href="/wiki/Catechism" title="Catechism">catechism</a>. During papal audiences, he would gather children around him and talk about things that interested them. His weekly catechism lessons in the courtyard of San Damaso in the Vatican always included a special place for children, and his decision to require the <a href="/wiki/Confraternity_of_Christian_Doctrine" title="Confraternity of Christian Doctrine">Confraternity of Christian Doctrine</a> in every parish was partly motivated by a desire to save children from religious ignorance.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Noted for his humility and simplicity, he declared that he had not changed personally save for his white cassock. Aides consistently needed to remind him not to wipe his pen on the white cassock, as he had previously done on his black cassock which hid stains. The new pope's schedule was quite similar each day. He rose at 4:00am before celebrating Mass at 6:00am. He was at his desk at 8:00am to receive private audiences. On his desk stood statues of <a href="/wiki/John_Vianney" title="John Vianney">John Vianney</a> and <a href="/wiki/Joan_of_Arc" title="Joan of Arc">Joan of Arc</a>, both of whom he beatified in his papacy. At noon, he conducted a general audience with pilgrims, then had lunch at 1:00pm with his two secretaries or whomever else he invited to dine with him. Resting for a short while after lunch, Pius X would then return to work before dining at 9:00pm and a final stint of work before sleep.<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Church_reforms_and_theology">Church reforms and theology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Church reforms and theology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Restoration_in_Christ_and_Mariology">Restoration in Christ and Mariology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Restoration in Christ and Mariology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In his 1904 encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Ad_diem_illum" title="Ad diem illum">Ad diem illum</a></i>, he views Mary in the context of "restoring everything in Christ".
</p><p>He wrote:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Spiritually we all are her children and she is the mother of us, therefore, she is to be revered like a mother.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESarto190410_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESarto190410-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Christ is the Word made Flesh and the Savior of mankind. He had a physical body like every other man: and as savior of the human family, he had a spiritual and mystical body, the Church. This, the Pope argues has consequences for our view of the Blessed Virgin. She did not conceive the Eternal Son of God merely that He might be made man taking His human nature from her, but also, by giving him her human nature, that He might be the Redeemer of men. Mary, carrying the Savior within her, also carried all those whose life was contained in the life of the Savior. Therefore, all the faithful united to Christ, are members of His body, of His flesh, and of His bones<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> from the womb of Mary like a body united to its head. Through a spiritual and mystical fashion, all are children of Mary, and she is their Mother. Mother, spiritually, but truly Mother of the members of Christ (S. Aug. L. de S. Virginitate, c. 6).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESarto190410_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESarto190410-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote><p>During Pius X's pontificate, many famed Marian images were granted a <a href="/wiki/Canonical_coronation" title="Canonical coronation">canonical coronation</a>: <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Aparecida" title="Our Lady of Aparecida">Our Lady of Aparecida</a>, <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_the_Pillar" title="Our Lady of the Pillar">Our Lady of the Pillar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_the_Cape" title="Our Lady of the Cape">Our Lady of the Cape</a>, <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_the_Rosary_of_Chiquinquir%C3%A1" title="Our Lady of the Rosary of Chiquinquirá">Our Lady of Chiquinquira of Colombia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_San_Juan_de_los_Lagos" title="Our Lady of San Juan de los Lagos">Our Lady of San Juan de los Lagos</a>, <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_La_Naval_de_Manila" title="Our Lady of La Naval de Manila">Our Lady of La Naval de Manila</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cathedral_Basilica_of_Our_Lady_of_Help_(Valencia,_Venezuela)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cathedral Basilica of Our Lady of Help (Valencia, Venezuela)">Virgin of Help of Venezuela</a>, <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Our_Lady_of_Mt._Carmel_(Manhattan)" class="mw-redirect" title="Church of Our Lady of Mt. Carmel (Manhattan)">Our Lady of Carmel of New York</a>, the Marian icon of <a href="/wiki/Santuario_della_Consolata" title="Santuario della Consolata">Santuario della Consolata</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a> within the <i>Chapel of the Choir</i> inside <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Basilica">Saint Peter's Basilica</a> were granted this prestigious honor.
</p><h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Tra_le_sollecitudini_and_Gregorian_chant"><i>Tra le sollecitudini</i> and Gregorian chant</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Tra le sollecitudini and Gregorian chant">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Within three months of his coronation, Pius X published his <i><a href="/wiki/Motu_proprio" title="Motu proprio">motu proprio</a></i> <i><a href="/wiki/Tra_le_sollecitudini" class="mw-redirect" title="Tra le sollecitudini">Tra le sollecitudini</a></i>. <a href="/wiki/European_classical_music" class="mw-redirect" title="European classical music">Classical</a> and <a href="/wiki/Baroque_music" title="Baroque music">Baroque</a> compositions had long been favoured over <a href="/wiki/Gregorian_chant" title="Gregorian chant">Gregorian chant</a> in ecclesiastical music.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> The Pope announced a return to earlier musical styles, championed by <a href="/wiki/Lorenzo_Perosi" title="Lorenzo Perosi">Lorenzo Perosi</a>. Since 1898, Perosi had been Director of the <a href="/wiki/Sistine_Chapel_Choir" title="Sistine Chapel Choir">Sistine Chapel Choir</a>, a title which Pius X upgraded to "Perpetual Director". The Pope's choice of <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Pothier" title="Joseph Pothier">Joseph Pothier</a> to supervise the new editions of chant led to the official adoption of the <a href="/wiki/Solesmes_Abbey" title="Solesmes Abbey">Solesmes</a> edition of Gregorian chant.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Liturgical_reforms_and_communion">Liturgical reforms and communion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Liturgical reforms and communion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Pius X worked to increase devotion among both clergy and <a href="/wiki/Laity" title="Laity">laity</a>, particularly in the <a href="/wiki/Breviary" title="Breviary">Breviary</a>, which he <a href="/wiki/Reform_of_the_Roman_Breviary_by_Pope_Pius_X" title="Reform of the Roman Breviary by Pope Pius X">reformed considerably</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">Mass</a>.
</p><p>Besides restoring to prominence Gregorian Chant, he placed a renewed <a href="/wiki/Liturgy" title="Liturgy">liturgical</a> emphasis on the <a href="/wiki/Eucharist" title="Eucharist">Eucharist</a>, saying, "Holy Communion is the shortest and safest way to Heaven." To this end, he encouraged frequent reception of Holy Communion. This also extended to children who had reached the "age of discretion", though he did not permit the ancient Eastern practice of <a href="/wiki/Infant_Communion" class="mw-redirect" title="Infant Communion">infant communion</a>. He also emphasized frequent recourse to the <a href="/wiki/Sacrament_of_Penance_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Sacrament of Penance (Catholic Church)">Sacrament of Penance</a> so that Holy Communion would be received worthily. Pius X's devotion to the Eucharist would eventually earn him the honorific of "Pope of the Blessed Sacrament", by which he is still known among his devotees.
</p><p>In 1910, he issued the decree <i><a href="/wiki/Quam_singulari" title="Quam singulari">Quam singulari</a></i>, which changed the age at which Communion could be received from 12 to 7 years old, the <a href="/wiki/Age_of_reason_(canon_law)" class="mw-redirect" title="Age of reason (canon law)">age of discretion</a>. The pope lowered the age because he wished to impress the event on the minds of children and stimulate their parents to new religious observance; this decree was found unwelcome in some places due to the belief that parents would withdraw their children early from Catholic schools, now that <a href="/wiki/First_Communion" title="First Communion">First Communion</a> was carried out earlier.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Pius X even personally distributed First Communion to a four-year-old boy the day after the child was presented to him and demonstrated an exceptional understanding of the meaning of the sacrament.<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> When people would criticize Pius X for lowering the age of reception, he simply quoted the words of Jesus, "let the little children come to me".
</p><p>Pius X said in his 1903 <i>motu proprio</i> <i>Tra le sollecitudini</i>, "The primary and indispensable source of the true Christian spirit is participation in the most holy mysteries and in the public, official prayer of the Church."<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He also sought to modify papal ceremonies to underscore their religious significance by eliminating occasions for applause. For example, when entering his first public consistory for the creation of cardinals in November 1903, he was not carried above the crowds on the <i><a href="/wiki/Sedia_gestatoria" title="Sedia gestatoria">sedia gestatoria</a></i> as was traditional. He arrived on foot wearing a cope and mitre at the end of the procession of prelates "almost hidden behind the double line of Palatine Guards through which he passed".<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Anti-modernism">Anti-modernism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Anti-modernism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>

</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content">
