</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-below plainlist" style="font-weight:normal; border-top:1px solid #CC0000; border-bottom:1px solid #CC0000;">
<ul><li><span class="mw-image-border noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:A_coloured_voting_box.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/01/A_coloured_voting_box.svg/16px-A_coloured_voting_box.svg.png" decoding="async" width="16" height="16" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/01/A_coloured_voting_box.svg/24px-A_coloured_voting_box.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/01/A_coloured_voting_box.svg/32px-A_coloured_voting_box.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="160" data-file-height="160" /></a></span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Portal:Politics" title="Portal:Politics">Politics&#32;portal</a></li></ul></td></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Integralism" title="Template:Integralism"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Integralism" title="Template talk:Integralism"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Integralism&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Pope Leo XIII had sought to revive the inheritance of <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Aquinas" title="Thomas Aquinas">Thomas Aquinas</a>, 'the marriage of reason and revelation', as a response to secular 'enlightenment'. Under Pius X, <i><a href="/wiki/Neo-Thomism" class="mw-redirect" title="Neo-Thomism">neo-Thomism</a></i> became the blueprint for theology.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENoel20098_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENoel20098-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Most controversially, Pius X vigorously condemned the theological movement he termed '<a href="/wiki/Modernism_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Modernism in the Catholic Church">Modernism</a>', which he regarded as a heresy endangering the <a href="/wiki/Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholicism">Catholic faith</a>. The movement was linked especially to certain Catholic French scholars such as <a href="/wiki/Louis_Duchesne" title="Louis Duchesne">Louis Duchesne</a>, who questioned the belief that God acts in a direct way in the affairs of humanity, and <a href="/wiki/Alfred_Loisy" title="Alfred Loisy">Alfred Loisy</a>, who denied that some parts of Scripture were literally rather than perhaps metaphorically true. In contradiction to Thomas Aquinas they argued that there was an unbridgeable gap between natural and supernatural knowledge. Its unwanted effects, from the traditional viewpoint, were relativism and scepticism.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Modernism and relativism, in terms of their presence in the Church, were theological trends that tried to assimilate modern philosophers like <a href="/wiki/Immanuel_Kant" title="Immanuel Kant">Immanuel Kant</a> as well as rationalism into Catholic theology.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Modernists argued that beliefs of the Church have evolved throughout its history and continue to evolve.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Anti-Modernists viewed these notions as contrary to the dogmas and traditions of the Catholic Church. In the decree entitled <i><a href="/wiki/Lamentabili_sane_exitu" title="Lamentabili sane exitu">Lamentabili sane exitu</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESarto1907_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESarto1907-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> ("A Lamentable Departure Indeed") of 3 July 1907, Pius X formally condemned 65 propositions, mainly drawn from the works of Alfred Loisy and concerning the nature of the Church, <a href="/wiki/Revelation" title="Revelation">revelation</a>, <a href="/wiki/Biblical_exegesis" class="mw-redirect" title="Biblical exegesis">biblical exegesis</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Sacraments" class="mw-redirect" title="Sacraments">sacraments</a>, and the divinity of Christ. That was followed by the encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Pascendi_dominici_gregis" class="mw-redirect" title="Pascendi dominici gregis">Pascendi dominici gregis</a></i> (or "Feeding the Lord's Flock"), which characterized Modernism as the "synthesis of all heresies." Following these, Pius X ordered that all clerics take the <a href="/wiki/Anti-Modernist_oath" class="mw-redirect" title="Anti-Modernist oath">Anti-Modernist oath</a>, <i>Sacrorum antistitum</i>.  Pius X's aggressive stance against Modernism caused some disruption within the Church. Although only about 40 clerics refused to take the oath, Catholic scholarship with Modernistic tendencies was substantially discouraged. Theologians who wished to pursue lines of inquiry in line with Secularism, Modernism, or Relativism had to stop or face conflict with the papacy, and possibly even <a href="/wiki/Excommunication" title="Excommunication">excommunication</a>.
</p><p>Pius X's attitude toward the Modernists was uncompromising. Speaking of those who counseled compassion, he said: "They want them to be treated with oil, soap and caresses. But they should be beaten with fists. In a duel, you don't count or measure the blows, you strike as you can."<sup id="cite_ref-home_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-home-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> He also instituted the <i><a href="/wiki/Sodalitium_Pianum" title="Sodalitium Pianum">Sodalitium Pianum</a></i> (or League of Pius V), an anti-Modernist network of informants, which was much criticized due to its accusations of heresy on the flimsiest evidence.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> This campaign was run by <a href="/wiki/Umberto_Benigni" title="Umberto Benigni">Umberto Benigni</a> in the Department of Extraordinary Affairs in the Secretariat of State, which distributed anti-Modernist propaganda and gathered information on "culprits". In Benigni's secret code, Pius X was known as <i>Mama</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-home_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-home-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:PiusXstudy.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/PiusXstudy.jpg/220px-PiusXstudy.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="154" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/PiusXstudy.jpg/330px-PiusXstudy.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/PiusXstudy.jpg/440px-PiusXstudy.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1297" data-file-height="910" /></a><figcaption>Pius X in his study while receiving a <a href="/wiki/Portrait" title="Portrait">portraiture</a>. Nearby is a statue of <a href="/wiki/John_Vianney" title="John Vianney">John Vianney</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Catechism_of_Saint_Pius_X"><i>Catechism of Saint Pius X</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Catechism of Saint Pius X">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Gala_Berlin_with_red_hammercloth.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Gala_Berlin_with_red_hammercloth.jpg/400px-Gala_Berlin_with_red_hammercloth.jpg" decoding="async" width="400" height="300" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Gala_Berlin_with_red_hammercloth.jpg/600px-Gala_Berlin_with_red_hammercloth.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Gala_Berlin_with_red_hammercloth.jpg/800px-Gala_Berlin_with_red_hammercloth.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="2736" /></a><figcaption>Gala Berlin produced in Rome by the Casalini brothers, renowned carriage manufacturers, during the papacy of Pius IX, whose coat of arms is painted on both doors. As shown by the emblems of Pius&#160;IX and Pius&#160;X, painted on the right and left doors, respectively, the carriage was used during various pontificates until the beginning of the twentieth century.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Catechism_of_Saint_Pius_X" title="Catechism of Saint Pius X">Catechism of Saint Pius X</a></div>
<p>In 1905, Pius X in his letter <i>Acerbo nimis</i> mandated the establishment of the Confraternity of Christian Doctrine (catechism class) in every parish in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <i>Catechism of Pius X</i> is his realisation of a simple, plain, brief, popular <a href="/wiki/Catechism" title="Catechism">catechism</a> for uniform use throughout the whole world; it was used in the ecclesiastical province of Rome and for some years in other parts of Italy; it was not, however, prescribed for use throughout the universal Church.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESarto19053_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESarto19053-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> The characteristics of Pius X were "simplicity of exposition and depth of content. Also because of this, Pius X's catechism might have friends in the future."<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Giorni_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Giorni-36">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> The catechism was extolled as a method of religious teaching in his encyclical <i>Acerbo nimis</i> of April 1905.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESarto1905a_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESarto1905a-37">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <i>Catechism of Saint Pius X</i> was issued in 1908 in Italian, as <i>Catechismo della dottrina Cristiana, Pubblicato per Ordine del Sommo Pontifice San Pio X</i>. An English translation runs to more than 115 pages.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESarto19052_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESarto19052-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Asked in 2003 whether the almost 100-year-old <i>Catechism of Saint Pius X</i> was still valid, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Joseph Ratzinger</a> said: "The faith as such is always the same. Hence the Catechism of Saint Pius X always preserves its value. Whereas ways of transmitting the contents of the faith can change instead. And hence one may wonder whether the Catechism of Saint Pius X can in that sense still be considered valid today."<sup id="cite_ref-Giorni_36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Giorni-36">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Reform_of_canon_law">Reform of canon law</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Reform of canon law">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1917_Code_of_Canon_Law" title="1917 Code of Canon Law">1917 Code of Canon Law</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Canon_law_of_the_Catholic_Church" title="Canon law of the Catholic Church">Canon law in the Catholic Church</a> varied from region to region with no overall prescriptions. On 19 March 1904, Pope Pius X named a commission of cardinals to draft a universal set of laws. Two of his successors worked in the commission: Giacomo della Chiesa, who became <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XV" title="Pope Benedict XV">Pope Benedict XV</a>, and Eugenio Pacelli, who became <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a>. This first <a href="/wiki/1917_Code_of_Canon_Law" title="1917 Code of Canon Law">Code of Canon Law</a> was promulgated by Benedict XV on 27 May 1917, with an effective date of 19 May 1918,<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> and remained in effect until Advent 1983.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Reform_of_Church_administration">Reform of Church administration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Reform of Church administration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Pius X reformed the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a> with the constitution <i><a href="/wiki/Sapienti_Consilio" class="mw-redirect" title="Sapienti Consilio">Sapienti Consilio</a></i> (29 June 1908)<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> and specified new rules enforcing a bishop's oversight of seminaries in the encyclical <i>Pieni l'animo</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> He established regional seminaries (closing some smaller ones), and promulgated a new plan of seminary study. He also barred clergy from administering social organizations.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Church_policies_towards_secular_governments">Church policies towards secular governments</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Church policies towards secular governments">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:MarrydelValpacelli.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/MarrydelValpacelli.jpg/220px-MarrydelValpacelli.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="304" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/MarrydelValpacelli.jpg/330px-MarrydelValpacelli.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/MarrydelValpacelli.jpg/440px-MarrydelValpacelli.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1090" data-file-height="1507" /></a><figcaption>Monsignor <a href="/wiki/Eugenio_Pacelli" class="mw-redirect" title="Eugenio Pacelli">Eugenio Pacelli</a> at left and Cardinal Secretary <a href="/wiki/Rafael_Merry_del_Val" title="Rafael Merry del Val">Rafael Merry del Val</a> at the signing ceremony of the <i>Serbian concordat</i> during the pontificate of Pius X, dated 24 June 1914</figcaption></figure><p>Pius X reversed the accommodating approach of Leo XIII towards secular governments, appointing <a href="/wiki/Rafael_Merry_del_Val" title="Rafael Merry del Val">Rafael Merry del Val</a> as Cardinal Secretary of State (Merry del Val would later have his own cause opened for canonization in 1953, but still has not been beatified<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>). When the French president <a href="/wiki/%C3%89mile_Loubet" title="Émile Loubet">Émile Loubet</a> visited the Italian monarch <a href="/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_III" class="mw-redirect" title="Victor Emmanuel III">Victor Emmanuel III</a> (1900–1946), Pius X, still refusing to accept the annexation of the papal territories by Italy, reproached the French president for the visit and refused to meet him. This led to a diplomatic break with France and to the 1905 <a href="/wiki/1905_French_law_on_the_Separation_of_the_Churches_and_the_State" title="1905 French law on the Separation of the Churches and the State">Law of Separation</a> between <a href="/wiki/Separation_of_Church_and_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Separation of Church and state">church and state</a>, by which the Church lost government funding in France. The pope denounced this law in his encyclicals <i><a href="/wiki/Vehementer_Nos" title="Vehementer Nos">Vehementer Nos</a></i> and <i>Gravissimo officii munere</i>, and removed two French bishops for recognising the <a href="/wiki/Third_Republic_(France)" class="mw-redirect" title="Third Republic (France)">Third Republic</a>. Eventually, France expelled the <a href="/wiki/Jesuits" title="Jesuits">Jesuits</a> and broke off diplomatic relations with the Vatican.
</p><p>The pope adopted a similar position toward secular governments in <a href="/wiki/First_Portuguese_Republic" title="First Portuguese Republic">Portugal</a>, <a href="/wiki/History_of_Ireland_(1801%E2%80%931923)" title="History of Ireland (1801–1923)">Ireland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Congress_Poland" title="Congress Poland">Poland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ethiopian_Empire" title="Ethiopian Empire">Ethiopia</a>, and in other states with large Catholic populations. His opposition to international relations with Italy angered the secular powers of these countries, as well as a few others like the UK and Russia. In Ireland, Protestants increasingly worried that a proposed <i><a href="/wiki/Irish_home_rule" class="mw-redirect" title="Irish home rule">Home Rule</a></i> by an Irish parliament representing the Catholic majority (rather than the <i>status quo</i> of rule by Westminster since the 1800 <a href="/wiki/Acts_of_Union_1800" title="Acts of Union 1800">Union of Ireland and Great Britain</a>) would result in <i><a href="/wiki/Rome_Rule" title="Rome Rule">Rome Rule</a></i> due to Pius X's uncompromising stance being followed by Irish Catholics (<a href="/wiki/Ultramontanism" title="Ultramontanism">Ultramontanism</a>).
</p><p>In 1908, the papal decree <i><a href="/wiki/Ne_Temere" title="Ne Temere">Ne Temere</a></i> came into effect which complicated <a href="/wiki/Interreligious_marriage" class="mw-redirect" title="Interreligious marriage">mixed marriages</a>. Marriages not performed by a Catholic priest were declared legal but sacramentally invalid, worrying some Protestants that the Church would counsel separation for couples married in a Protestant church or by civil service.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Priests were given discretion to refuse mixed marriages or to lay conditions upon them, commonly including a requirement that the children be raised Catholic. The decree proved particularly divisive in Ireland, which its large Protestant minority, contributing indirectly to the subsequent political conflict there and provoking debates in the British <a href="/wiki/House_of_Commons_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="House of Commons of the United Kingdom">House of Commons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> The long term effect of <i>Ne Temere</i> in Ireland was that <a href="/wiki/Irish_Unionism" class="mw-redirect" title="Irish Unionism">Irish Unionism</a> which had had strongholds in Dublin as well as <a href="/wiki/Ulster" title="Ulster">Ulster</a>, but existed to some extent on the entire island of Ireland, declined overall and became virtually exclusively a phenomenon of what is today <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland" title="Northern Ireland">Northern Ireland</a>. Furthermore, while historically both <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Irish_nationalists" title="Protestant Irish nationalists">Protestant Irish nationalists</a> and <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Unionist" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic Unionist">Catholic Unionists</a> existed, the split over who should rule Northern Ireland eventually came to almost exactly match the confessional divide.
</p><p>As secular authority challenged the papacy, Pius X became more aggressive. He suspended the <i><a href="/wiki/Opera_dei_Congressi" title="Opera dei Congressi">Opera dei Congressi</a></i>, which coordinated the work of Catholic associations in Italy, as well as condemning <i><a href="/wiki/Le_Sillon" title="Le Sillon">Le Sillon</a></i>, a French social movement that tried to reconcile the Church with liberal political views. He also opposed <a href="/wiki/Trade_union" title="Trade union">trade unions</a> that were not exclusively Catholic.
</p><p>Pius X partially lifted <a href="/wiki/Non_Expedit" title="Non Expedit">decrees prohibiting</a> Italian Catholics from voting, but he never recognised the Italian government.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Relations_with_the_Kingdom_of_Italy">Relations with the Kingdom of Italy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Relations with the Kingdom of Italy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Initially, Pius maintained his <a href="/wiki/Prisoner_in_the_Vatican" title="Prisoner in the Vatican">prisoner in the Vatican</a> stance, but with the rise of socialism he began to allow the <i><a href="/wiki/Non_Expedit" title="Non Expedit">Non Expedit</a></i>, which prohibited Catholics from voting, to be relaxed. In 1905, he authorized bishops in his <a href="/wiki/Encyclical" title="Encyclical">encyclical</a> <a href="/w/index.php?title=Il_fermo_proposito&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Il fermo proposito (page does not exist)"><i>Il fermo proposito</i></a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Il_fermo_proposito" class="extiw" title="de:Il fermo proposito">de</a>; <a href="https://it.wikipedia.org/wiki/Il_Fermo_Proposito" class="extiw" title="it:Il Fermo Proposito">it</a>; <a href="https://la.wikipedia.org/wiki/Il_fermo_proposito" class="extiw" title="la:Il fermo proposito">la</a>&#93;</span> to offer a dispensation allowing their parishioners to exercise their legislative rights when "the supreme good of society" was at stake.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Relations_with_Poland_and_Russia">Relations with Poland and Russia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Relations with Poland and Russia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X_and_Russia" title="Pope Pius X and Russia">Pope Pius X and Russia</a></div>
<p>Under Pius X, the traditionally difficult situation of Polish Catholics in Russia did not improve. Although <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_II_of_Russia" title="Nicholas II of Russia">Nicholas II of Russia</a> issued a decree 22 February 1903, promising religious freedom for the Catholic Church, and in 1905 promulgated a constitution which included religious freedom,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlinde_Waal1904125_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlinde_Waal1904125-46">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodox Church</a> felt threatened and insisted on stiff interpretations. <a href="/wiki/Papal_decree" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal decree">Papal decrees</a> were not permitted and contacts with the Vatican remained outlawed.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Activities_for_the_United_States">Activities for the United States</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Activities for the United States">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 1908, Pius X lifted the United States out of its missionary status, in recognition of the growth of the American Church.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Fifteen new dioceses were created in the US during his pontificate, and he named two American cardinals. He was very popular among American Catholics, often depicted as an ordinary man from a poor family, raised by God to the papal throne.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1910, the pope refused an audience with former <a href="/wiki/Vice_President_of_the_United_States" title="Vice President of the United States">Vice-President</a> <a href="/wiki/Charles_W._Fairbanks" title="Charles W. Fairbanks">Charles W. Fairbanks</a>, who had addressed the <a href="/wiki/Methodism" title="Methodism">Methodist</a> association in Rome, as well as with former <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_United_States" title="President of the United States">President</a> <a href="/wiki/Theodore_Roosevelt" title="Theodore Roosevelt">Theodore Roosevelt</a>, who intended to address the same association.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 8 July 1914, Pope Pius X approved the request of <a href="/wiki/Cardinalate" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinalate">Cardinal</a> <a href="/wiki/James_Gibbons" title="James Gibbons">James Gibbons</a> to invoke the <a href="/wiki/Patronages_of_the_Immaculate_Conception" title="Patronages of the Immaculate Conception">patronage of the Immaculate Conception</a> for the construction site of the <a href="/wiki/National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception" class="mw-redirect" title="National Shrine of the Immaculate Conception">National Shrine of the Immaculate Conception</a> in <a href="/wiki/Washington,_DC" class="mw-redirect" title="Washington, DC">Washington, DC</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Miracles_during_the_pope.27s_lifetime"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Miracles_during_the_pope's_lifetime">Miracles during the pope's lifetime</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Miracles during the pope&#039;s lifetime">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Other than the stories of miracles performed through the pope's intercession after his death, there are also stories of miracles performed by the pope during his lifetime.
On one occasion, during a papal audience, Pius X was holding a paralyzed child who wriggled free from his arms and then ran around the room. On another occasion, a couple (who had made confession to him while he was bishop of Mantua) with a two-year-old child with meningitis wrote to the pope and Pius X then wrote back to them to hope and pray. Two days later, the child was cured.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Ernesto_Ruffini" title="Ernesto Ruffini">Ernesto Ruffini</a> (later the Archbishop of Palermo) had visited the pope after Ruffini was diagnosed with tuberculosis, and the pope had told him to go back to the seminary and that he would be fine. Ruffini gave this story to the investigators of the pontiff's cause for canonization.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Once, a man who suffered from a paralyzed arm begged Pius X for his help. Taking his arm in his hand, the pope simply said, "have confidence in the Lord ... only have faith and the Lord will heal you". At that moment, the man could actually move his arm, calling out to the pope joyfully who simply put a finger to his lips so as not to draw any attention to what had happened, indicating that the man simply hold his peace.<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Another case saw an Irish girl covered in sores taken to see the pope by her mother. When Pius X saw her, he placed his hand on her head, and the sores completely disappeared. Another case saw a Roman schoolgirl contract a serious foot disease that rendered her crippled since she was only a year old. Through a friend she managed to acquire one of the pope's socks and was told that she would be healed if she wore it, which she did. At the moment she placed the sock on, the diseased foot was instantly healed. When Pius X heard about this, he laughed and said, "What a joke! I wear my own socks every day and still I suffer from constant pain in my feet!"<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_activities">Other activities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Other activities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg/220px-PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="235" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg/330px-PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg/440px-PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1220" data-file-height="1301" /></a><figcaption>Pius X consecrates Bishop Giacomo Paolo Giovanni Battista della Chiesa, the future <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XV" title="Pope Benedict XV">Pope Benedict XV</a>, in the Vatican in 1907.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_encyclicals_of_Pope_Pius_X" title="List of encyclicals of Pope Pius X">List of encyclicals of Pope Pius X</a></div>
<p>In addition to the political defense of the Church, liturgical reforms, anti-modernism, and the beginning of the codification of canon law, the papacy of Pius X saw the reorganisation of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a>. He also sought to update the education of priests, seminaries and their curricula were reformed. In 1904 Pope Pius X granted permission for diocesan seminarians to attend the College of St. Thomas. He raised the college to the status of <i>Pontificium</i> on 2 May 1906, thus making its degrees equivalent to those of the world's other pontifical universities.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERenz2009&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidt8qt63uOg6ICpgPA43_43&#93;_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERenz2009[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidt8qt63uOg6ICpgPA43_43]-49">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> By <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Letter" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Letter">Apostolic Letter</a> of 8 November 1908, signed by the Supreme Pontiff on 17 November, the college was transformed into the <i>Collegium Pontificium Internationale Angelicum</i>. It would become the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_University_of_Saint_Thomas_Aquinas" title="Pontifical University of Saint Thomas Aquinas">Pontifical University of Saint Thomas Aquinas, <i>Angelicum</i></a> in 1963.
</p><p>Pius X published 16 encyclicals; among them was <i><a href="/wiki/Vehementer_nos" class="mw-redirect" title="Vehementer nos">Vehementer nos</a></i> on 11 February 1906, which condemned the <a href="/wiki/1905_French_law_on_the_separation_of_the_State_and_the_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="1905 French law on the separation of the State and the Church">1905 French law on the separation of the State and the Church</a>. Pius X also confirmed, though not infallibly,<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> the existence of <a href="/wiki/Limbo" title="Limbo">Limbo</a> in Catholic theology in his 1905 <a href="/wiki/Catechism#Catholic_catechisms" title="Catechism">Catechism</a>, saying that the unbaptized "do not have the joy of God but neither do they suffer... they do not deserve Paradise, but neither do they deserve Hell or <a href="/wiki/Purgatory" title="Purgatory">Purgatory</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> On 23 November 1903, Pius X issued a papal directive, a <i><a href="/wiki/Motu_proprio" title="Motu proprio">motu proprio</a></i>, that banned women from singing in church <a href="/wiki/Choir_(architecture)" title="Choir (architecture)">choirs</a> (i.e. the architectural choir).
</p><p>In the <a href="/wiki/Prophecy_of_the_Popes" title="Prophecy of the Popes">Prophecy of St. Malachy</a>, the collection of 112 prophecies about the popes, Pius X appears as <i>Ignis Ardens</i> or "Ardent Fire".
</p>
<h4><span id="Declaring_the_tango_.22off-limits.22"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Declaring_the_tango_&quot;off-limits&quot;">Declaring the tango "off-limits"</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Declaring the tango &quot;off-limits&quot;">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In November 1913, Pope Pius X declared <a href="/wiki/Tango" title="Tango">tango dancing</a> as immoral and off-limits to Catholics.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonizations_and_beatifications">Canonizations and beatifications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Canonizations and beatifications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_people_beatified_by_Pope_Pius_X" title="List of people beatified by Pope Pius X">List of people beatified by Pope Pius X</a></div>
<p>Pius X <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatified</a> a total of 131 individuals (including groups of martyrs and those by recognition of "cultus") and <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> four. Those beatified during his pontificate included <a href="/wiki/Marie-Genevi%C3%A8ve_Meunier" class="mw-redirect" title="Marie-Geneviève Meunier">Marie-Geneviève Meunier</a> (1906), <a href="/wiki/Rose-Chr%C3%A9tien_de_la_Neuville" title="Rose-Chrétien de la Neuville">Rose-Chrétien de la Neuville</a> (1906), Valentin de Berriochoa (1906), <a href="/wiki/Clair_of_Nantes" title="Clair of Nantes">Clair of Nantes</a> (1907), <a href="/wiki/Zdislava_Berka" title="Zdislava Berka">Zdislava Berka</a> (1907), <a href="/wiki/John_Bosco" title="John Bosco">John Bosco</a> (1907), <a href="/wiki/John_of_Ruysbroeck" class="mw-redirect" title="John of Ruysbroeck">John of Ruysbroeck</a> (1908), <a href="/w/index.php?title=Andrew_Nam_Thung&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Andrew Nam Thung (page does not exist)">Andrew Nam Thung</a> (1909), <a href="/wiki/Agatha_Lin" title="Agatha Lin">Agatha Lin</a> (1909), <a href="/w/index.php?title=Agnes_De&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Agnes De (page does not exist)">Agnes De</a> (1909), <a href="/wiki/Joan_of_Arc" title="Joan of Arc">Joan of Arc</a> (1909), and <a href="/wiki/John_Eudes" title="John Eudes">John Eudes</a> (1909). Those canonized by him were <a href="/wiki/Alexander_Sauli" title="Alexander Sauli">Alexander Sauli</a> (1904), <a href="/wiki/Gerard_Majella" title="Gerard Majella">Gerard Majella</a> (1904), <a href="/wiki/Clement_Mary_Hofbauer" title="Clement Mary Hofbauer">Clement Mary Hofbauer</a> (1909), and <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Oriol" title="Joseph Oriol">Joseph Oriol</a> (1909).
</p><p>In 1908 Pope Pius X named <a href="/wiki/John_Chrysostom" title="John Chrysostom">John Chrysostom</a> a patron saint of preachers.<sup id="cite_ref-episcopal_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-episcopal-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Consistories">Consistories</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Consistories">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Pius_X" title="Cardinals created by Pius X">Cardinals created by Pius X</a></div>
<p>Pius X created 50 cardinals in seven consistories held during his pontificate which included noted figures of the Church during that time such as <a href="/wiki/D%C3%A9sir%C3%A9-Joseph_Mercier" title="Désiré-Joseph Mercier">Désiré-Joseph Mercier</a> (1907) and <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Gasparri" title="Pietro Gasparri">Pietro Gasparri</a> (1907). In 1911, he increased American representation in the cardinalate based on the fact that the United States was expanding; the pope also named one cardinal <i><a href="/wiki/In_pectore" title="In pectore">in pectore</a></i> (<a href="/wiki/Ant%C3%B3nio_Mendes_Belo" title="António Mendes Belo">António Mendes Belo</a>, whom the media accurately speculated on) in 1911 whose name he later revealed in 1914. Pius X also named as a cardinal <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XV" title="Pope Benedict XV">Giacomo della Chiesa</a>, his immediate successor, Pope Benedict XV.
</p><p>Among the cardinals whom he nominated came the first Brazilian-born (and the first Latin American-born; <a href="/wiki/Joaquim_Arcoverde_de_Albuquerque_Cavalcanti" title="Joaquim Arcoverde de Albuquerque Cavalcanti">Arcoverde</a>) and the first from the Netherlands (<a href="/wiki/Willem_Marinus_van_Rossum" title="Willem Marinus van Rossum">van Rossum</a>) since 1523. The consistory of 1911 was the largest number of cardinals elevated at a single consistory in roughly a century.
</p><p>In 1911, the pope reportedly wished to elevate Diomede Panici to the cardinalate; however, Panici died before the promotion ever took place. Furthermore, this came after Panici was originally considered but passed for the elevation by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a> who even had considered elevating Panici's brother. In the 1914 consistory, Pius X considered naming the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Friars_Minor_Capuchin" title="Order of Friars Minor Capuchin">Capuchin</a> friar Armando Pietro Sabadel to the cardinalate; however, Sabadel declined the pope's invitation.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death_and_burial">Death and burial</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Death and burial">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:PiusXAug201914.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/PiusXAug201914.jpg/220px-PiusXAug201914.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="269" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/PiusXAug201914.jpg/330px-PiusXAug201914.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/PiusXAug201914.jpg/440px-PiusXAug201914.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1281" data-file-height="1569" /></a><figcaption>Pius X on his deathbed</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1913, Pope Pius X suffered a heart attack, and subsequently lived in the shadow of poor health. In 1914, the pope fell ill on the <a href="/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary" title="Assumption of Mary">Feast of the Assumption of Mary</a> (15 August 1914), an illness from which he would not recover, and it was reported that he suffered from a fever and lung complications. His condition was worsened by the events leading to the outbreak of <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a> (1914–1918), which reportedly sent the 79-year-old into a state of melancholy. He died on Thursday, 20 August 1914, only a few hours after the death of <a href="/wiki/Superior_General_of_the_Society_of_Jesus" title="Superior General of the Society of Jesus">Jesuit leader</a> <a href="/wiki/Franz_Xavier_Wernz" title="Franz Xavier Wernz">Franz Xavier Wernz</a>, and on the very day when German forces marched into <a href="/wiki/Brussels" title="Brussels">Brussels</a>.
</p><p>While the pope's condition was classified as serious, alarming symptoms did not develop until 19 August. While Pius X spent most of that day in and out of consciousness, he said at one stage, "In ancient times, the pope by a word might have stayed the slaughter, but now he is impotent". The pope shortly after received the Last Rites and eventually, an old bronchial infection that the pope had previously had in years past returned, further hastening his decline.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius X died at 1:20am on 20 August 1914 and in a moment of lucidity just moments before he died, Pius X is reported to have said: "Now I begin to think the end is approaching. The Almighty in His inexhaustible goodness wishes to spare me the horrors which Europe is undergoing".<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> His sister, Anna, was praying at his bedside when Pius X died. The pope's last act of life was to kiss the little crucifix that he had clasped in his hands at around 1:15am.<sup id="cite_ref-SPX_12-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPX-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius X was buried in a <a href="/wiki/List_of_papal_tombs" class="mw-redirect" title="List of papal tombs">simple and unadorned tomb</a> in the crypt below <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Basilica">Saint Peter's Basilica</a>. His body was <a href="/wiki/Lying_in_state" title="Lying in state">laid in state</a> on 21 August in red pontifical vestments and then interred following the <a href="/wiki/Requiem" title="Requiem">Requiem Mass</a> following his coffin lying in state on a large <a href="/wiki/Catafalque" title="Catafalque">catafalque</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Sistine_Chapel" title="Sistine Chapel">Sistine Chapel</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-SSPX_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SSPX-58">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> His original tombstone bore the inscription: "Pope Pius X, poor and yet rich, gentle and humble of heart, unconquerable champion of the Catholic Faith, whose constant endeavor it was to renew all things in Christ".<sup id="cite_ref-SPC_18-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SPC-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Papal physicians had been in the habit of removing organs to aid the embalming process. Pius X expressly prohibited this in his burial and successive popes have continued this tradition. Pius X's tomb is located near the tombs of both <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">Pope John XXIII</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> under the altar of the Presentation.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Exhumation">Exhumation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Exhumation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 19 May 1944, the body of Pius X was exhumed for inspection as part of the beatification process, during which the remains were found to be miraculously incorrupt. In 1959, the remains of the late pope were sent, with the permission of <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">Pope John XXIII</a> (himself a former Patriarch of Venice), to <a href="/wiki/Venice" title="Venice">Venice</a> in 1959. Before the remains were taken to Venice via a train on 11 April, Pope John XXIII led a small prayer service alongside some other cardinals. The body was exposed for the veneration of the faithful at the Basilica of Saint Mark for one month (12 April–10 May) before it was returned to the late pope's original resting place, with Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Urbani" title="Giovanni Urbani">Giovanni Urbani</a> acting as the papal legate for the month-long event.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonization">Canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above n" style="background-color:gold;"><div style="display:inline;" class="fn">Pope Saint Pius X</div></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><span class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Frameless"><a class="bday">1835-06-02</span>)</span>2 June 1835<br />Riese, Treviso, Lombardy-Venetia, Austrian Empire</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Died</th><td class="infobox-data">20 August 1914<span style="display:none">(1914-08-20)</span> (aged&#160;79)<br />Apostolic Palace, Rome, Kingdom of Italy</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Venerated&#160;in</th><td class="infobox-data">Catholic Church</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">Beatified</a></th><td class="infobox-data">3 June 1951, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Basilica">Saint Peter's Basilica</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">Canonized</a></th><td class="infobox-data">29 May 1954, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Basilica">Saint Peter's Basilica</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City</a> by Pope Pius XII</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">Feast</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/21_August" class="mw-redirect" title="21 August">21 August</a><br /><a href="/wiki/3_September" class="mw-redirect" title="3 September">3 September</a> (General Roman Calendar 1955–1969)</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">Patronage</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Atlanta" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Atlanta">Archdiocese of Atlanta, Georgia</a>; <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Des_Moines" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Des Moines">diocese of Des Moines, Iowa</a>; first communicants; <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Great_Falls-Billings" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Great Falls-Billings">Diocese of Great Falls-Billings, Montana</a>; <a href="/wiki/Changanassery#Archdiocese_of_Changanacherry" title="Changanassery">archdiocese of Kottayam, India</a>; pilgrims; <a href="/wiki/Santa_Lu%C4%8Bija" title="Santa Luċija">Santa Luċija, Malta</a>; <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Springfield-Cape_Girardeau" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Springfield-Cape Girardeau">Diocese of Springfield-Cape Girardeau, Missouri</a>; <a href="/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Zamboanga" class="mw-redirect" title="Archdiocese of Zamboanga">Archdiocese of Zamboanga, Philippines</a>; emigrants from Treviso; <a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_Venice" title="Patriarch of Venice">Patriarchate of Venice</a>; <a href="/wiki/St._Pius_X_Seminary_(Dubuque,_Iowa)" title="St. Pius X Seminary (Dubuque, Iowa)">St. Pius X Seminary (Dubuque, Iowa)</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:0_Statue_de_Pie_X_-_Basilique_St-Pierre_-_Vatican.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/0_Statue_de_Pie_X_-_Basilique_St-Pierre_-_Vatican.jpg/170px-0_Statue_de_Pie_X_-_Basilique_St-Pierre_-_Vatican.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/0_Statue_de_Pie_X_-_Basilique_St-Pierre_-_Vatican.jpg/255px-0_Statue_de_Pie_X_-_Basilique_St-Pierre_-_Vatican.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/0_Statue_de_Pie_X_-_Basilique_St-Pierre_-_Vatican.jpg/340px-0_Statue_de_Pie_X_-_Basilique_St-Pierre_-_Vatican.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2487" data-file-height="3730" /></a><figcaption>The statue of Pius X in St. Peter's Basilica</figcaption></figure>
<p>Although Pius X's canonization took place in 1954, the events leading up to it began immediately with his death. A letter of 24 September 1916 by Monsignor Leo, <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Nicotera_and_Tropea" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Nicotera and Tropea">Bishop of Nicotera and Tropea</a>, referred to Pius X as "a great Saint and a great Pope." To accommodate the large number of pilgrims seeking access to his tomb, more than the crypt would hold, "a small metal cross was set into the floor of the basilica," which read <i>Pius Papa X</i>, "so that the faithful might kneel down directly above the tomb".<sup id="cite_ref-angelusonline.org_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-angelusonline.org-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Masses were held near his tomb until 1930.
</p><p>Devotion to Pius X between the two <a href="/wiki/World_war" title="World war">world wars</a> remained high. On 14 February 1923, in honor of the 20th anniversary of his accession to the papacy, the first moves toward his <a href="/wiki/Canonisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonisation">canonization</a> began with the formal appointment of those who would carry out his cause. The event was marked by the erecting of a monument in his memory in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's Basilica</a>. On 19 August 1939, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> (1939–58) delivered a tribute to Pius X at <a href="/wiki/Castel_Gandolfo" title="Castel Gandolfo">Castel Gandolfo</a>. On 12 February 1943, a further development of Pius X's cause was achieved, when he was declared to have displayed <a href="/wiki/Heroic_virtue" title="Heroic virtue">heroic virtues</a>, gaining therefore the title "Venerable".
</p><p>On 19 May 1944, Pius X's coffin was exhumed and was taken to the Chapel of the Holy Crucifix in St. Peter's Basilica for the canonical examination.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
Upon opening the coffin, the examiners found the body of Pius X remarkably well preserved, despite the fact that he had died 30 years before and had made wishes not to be embalmed. According to Jerome Dai-Gal, "all of the body" of Pius X "was in an excellent state of conservation".<sup id="cite_ref-angelusonline.org_60-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-angelusonline.org-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> At the canonical recognition of his mortal body were present the Italian cardinals <a href="/wiki/Alfredo_Ottaviani" title="Alfredo Ottaviani">Alfredo Ottaviani</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nicola_Canali" title="Nicola Canali">Nicola Canali</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After the examination and the end of the apostolic process towards Pius X's cause, Pius XII bestowed the title of <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a> Servant of God upon Pius X. His body was exposed for 45 days (Rome was liberated by the Allies during this time), before being placed back in his tomb.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Pius_X_during_his_lying_in_state.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Pope_Pius_X_during_his_lying_in_state.jpg/220px-Pope_Pius_X_during_his_lying_in_state.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="125" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Pope_Pius_X_during_his_lying_in_state.jpg/330px-Pope_Pius_X_during_his_lying_in_state.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Pope_Pius_X_during_his_lying_in_state.jpg/440px-Pope_Pius_X_during_his_lying_in_state.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="570" /></a><figcaption>Pius X during his lying in state, 21–22 August 1914</figcaption></figure>
<p>Following this, the process towards <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a> began, and investigations by the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Sacred Congregation of Rites</a> (SCR) into <a href="/wiki/Miracle" title="Miracle">miracles</a> performed by intercessory work of Pius X took place. The SCR would eventually recognize two miracles. The first involved Marie-Françoise Deperras, a nun who had <a href="/wiki/Bone_cancer" class="mw-redirect" title="Bone cancer">bone cancer</a> and was cured on 7 December 1928 during a <a href="/wiki/Novena" title="Novena">novena</a> in which a relic of Pius X was placed on her chest. The second involved the nun Benedetta De Maria, who had cancer, and in a novena started in 1938, she eventually touched a relic statue of Pius X and was cured.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> officially approved the two miracles on 11 February 1951; and on 4 March, Pius XII, in his <i>De Tuto</i>, declared that the Church could continue in the beatification of Pius X. His beatification took place on 3 June 1951 at St. Peter's before 23 cardinals, hundreds of bishops and archbishops, and a crowd of 100,000 faithful. During his beatification decree, Pius XII referred to Pius X as "Pope of the Eucharist", in honor of Pius X's expansion of the rite to children.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Piusxtomb.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Piusxtomb.jpg/220px-Piusxtomb.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Piusxtomb.jpg/330px-Piusxtomb.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Piusxtomb.jpg/440px-Piusxtomb.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2592" data-file-height="1944" /></a><figcaption>The tomb of Pope Pius X under the Presentation chapel altar in St. Peter's Basilica</figcaption></figure>
<p>Following his beatification, on 17 February 1952, Pius X's body was transferred from its tomb to the Vatican basilica and placed under the altar of the chapel of the Presentation. The pontiff's body lies within a glass and bronze-work sarcophagus for the faithful to see.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 29 May 1954, less than three years after his beatification, Pius X was canonized, following the SCR's recognition of two more miracles. The first involved Francesco Belsami, an attorney from <a href="/wiki/Naples" title="Naples">Naples</a> who had a <a href="/wiki/Human_lung" class="mw-redirect" title="Human lung">pulmonary</a> <a href="/wiki/Abscess" title="Abscess">abscess</a>, who was cured upon placing a picture of Pope Pius X upon his chest. The second miracle involved Sr. Maria Ludovica Scorcia, a nun who was afflicted with a serious <a href="/wiki/Nervous_system" title="Nervous system">neurotropic</a> virus, and who, upon several novenas, was entirely cured. The canonization Mass was presided over by Pius XII at Saint Peter's Basilica before a crowd of about 800,000<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> of the faithful and Church officials at St. Peter's Basilica. Pius X became the first pope to be canonized since <a href="/wiki/Pius_V" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius V">Pius V</a> in 1712.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His canonization ceremony was taped and recorded by early <a href="/wiki/Television" title="Television">television</a> news broadcasters, including <a href="/wiki/NBC" title="NBC">NBC</a>.
</p><p>Prayer cards often depict the sanctified pontiff with instruments of <a href="/wiki/Eucharist" title="Eucharist">Holy Communion</a>. In addition to being celebrated as the "Pope of the Blessed Sacrament", Pius X is also the patron saint of emigrants from Treviso. He is honored in numerous parishes in Italy, Germany, Belgium, Canada, and the United States.
</p><p>The number of parishes, schools, seminaries and retreat houses named after him in Western countries is very large, partly because he was very well known, and his beatification and canonization in the early 1950s was during a period of time following <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a> when there was a great deal of new construction in cities and population growth in the era of the baby boom, thus leading to Catholic institutional expansion that correlated with the growing society.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius X's feast day was assigned in 1955 to 3 September, to be celebrated as a Double. It remained thus for 15 years. In the 1960 calendar, the rank was changed to Third-Class Feast. The rank in the General Roman Calendar since 1969 is that of <a href="/wiki/Memorial_(liturgy)" title="Memorial (liturgy)">Memorial</a> and the feast day is obligatorily celebrated on 21 August, closer to the day of his death (20 August, impeded by the feast day of St Bernard).<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Confraternity_of_Christian_Doctrine" title="Confraternity of Christian Doctrine">Confraternity of Christian Doctrine</a> was a big supporter of his canonization, partly because he had ordained the need for its existence in every diocese and because it had received a great deal of episcopal criticism, and it was thought that by canonizing the pope who gave them their mandate, this would help inoculate against this criticism.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> They initiated a prayer crusade for his canonization that achieved the participation of over two million names.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After the Pope's canonization, another miracle is said to have taken place when a Christian family activist named Clem Lane suffered a major heart attack and was placed in an oxygen tent, where he was given extreme unction. A relic of the Pope was placed over his tent, and he recovered to the great surprise of his doctors.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> A sister of Loretto at Webster College in St Louis, Missouri, claimed that her priest brother had been cured through the Pope's intercession as well.<sup id="cite_ref-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80_23-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steven_M_1997_pp.57-80-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Papal_coat_of_arms">Papal coat of arms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Papal coat of arms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:C_o_a_Pius_X.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/C_o_a_Pius_X.svg/170px-C_o_a_Pius_X.svg.png" decoding="async" width="170" height="248" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/C_o_a_Pius_X.svg/255px-C_o_a_Pius_X.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/C_o_a_Pius_X.svg/340px-C_o_a_Pius_X.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="889" data-file-height="1297" /></a><figcaption>Coat of arms of Pius X</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Papal_coats_of_arms" title="Papal coats of arms">personal papal arms</a> of Pius X are composed of the traditional elements of all papal <a href="/wiki/Heraldry" title="Heraldry">heraldry</a> before <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a>: the <a href="/wiki/Escutcheon_(heraldry)" title="Escutcheon (heraldry)">shield</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Papal_tiara" title="Papal tiara">papal tiara</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Papal_regalia_and_insignia" title="Papal regalia and insignia">keys</a>. The tiara and keys are typical symbols used in the <a href="/wiki/Coats_of_arms" class="mw-redirect" title="Coats of arms">coats of arms</a> of pontiffs, which symbolize their authority.
</p><p>The shield of Pius X's coat of arms is charged in two basic parts, as it is <i>per fess</i>. In chief (the top part of the shield) shows the arms of the <a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_Venice" title="Patriarch of Venice">Patriarch of Venice</a>, which Pius X was from 1893 to 1903. It consists of the <a href="/wiki/Lion_of_Saint_Mark" title="Lion of Saint Mark">Lion of Saint Mark</a> proper and haloed in silver upon a silver-white background, displaying a book with the inscription of <i><small>PAX TIBI MARCE</small></i> on the left page and <i><small>EVANGELISTA MEUS</small></i> on the right page.
</p><p><i>"Pax tibi Marce Evangelista Meus"</i> (English: <i>Peace to you, Mark, my evangelist</i>) is the motto of <a href="/wiki/Venice" title="Venice">Venice</a> referring to the final resting place of <a href="/wiki/Mark_the_Evangelist" title="Mark the Evangelist">Mark the Evangelist</a>. This differed from the arms of the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Venice" title="Republic of Venice">Republic of Venice</a> by changing the background color from red to silver even though that did not conform to heraldic rules.<sup id="cite_ref-Papal_Heraldry_page_105_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Papal_Heraldry_page_105-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Previous Patriarchs of Venice had combined their personal arms with these arms of the Patriarchate.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> The same <i>chief</i> can be seen in the arms of the later popes who were Patriarchs of Venice upon election to the See of Rome, John XXIII and John Paul I. Renditions of this part of Pius X's arms depict the lion either with or without a sword, and sometimes only one side of the book is written on.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The shield displays the arms Pius X took as <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Mantua" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Mantua">Bishop of Mantua</a>: an anchor proper cast into a stormy sea (the blue and silver wavy lines), lit up by a single six-pointed star of gold.<sup id="cite_ref-Papal_Heraldry_page_105_68-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Papal_Heraldry_page_105-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> These were inspired by <a href="/wiki/Book_of_Hebrews" class="mw-redirect" title="Book of Hebrews">Hebrews</a> Chapter 6, Versicle 19, (English: "The hope we have is the sure and steadfast anchor of the soul") As Bishop Sarto, he stated that "hope is the sole companion of my life, the greatest support in uncertainty, the strongest power in situations of weakness."<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Although not present upon his arms, the only motto attributed to Pope Pius X is the one for which he is best remembered: <i>Instaurare omnia in Christo</i> (English: "To restore all things in Christ"),<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> allegedly his last words before his death.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_fiction">In fiction</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: In fiction">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The life of Pope Pius X is depicted in the 1951 movie <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gli_uomini_non-guardano_il_cielo&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Gli uomini non-guardano il cielo (page does not exist)">Gli uomini non-guardano il cielo</a></i> by Umberto Scarpelli. The movie is centered on the year 1914, as the Pope grieves over the threat that is incumbent and is consoled by his nephew.
</p><p>A satirical depiction of Pope Pius X is presented in <a href="/wiki/Flann_O%27Brien" title="Flann O&#39;Brien">Flann O'Brien</a>'s novel <i><a href="/wiki/The_Hard_Life" title="The Hard Life">The Hard Life</a></i>, as the Irish characters travel from Dublin to Rome and gain a personal interview with the Pope, which ends very badly.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_poetry">In poetry</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: In poetry">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In the poem "Zone" by <a href="/wiki/Guillaume_Apollinaire" title="Guillaume Apollinaire">Guillaume Apollinaire</a>, Pope Pius X is referred to as "L'Européen le plus moderne,"<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> translated into English as "the most modern European."<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-4"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-4">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><small>Italian:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="it-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Italian" title="Help:IPA/Italian">[dʒuˈzɛppe melˈkjɔrre ˈsarto]</a></span>; English: <b>Joseph Melchior Sarto</b></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-9"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-9">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Prior to the changes of the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a>, the celebration of the Mass did not always necessitate a <a href="/wiki/Sermon" title="Sermon">sermon</a> or <a href="/wiki/Homily" title="Homily">homily</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-25"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-25">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a> revived the practice of dining alone during his pontificate but no other successor of Pius X is known to have followed this custom.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="After_his_death">After his death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_X&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: After his death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFF._A._Forbes1924" class="citation book cs1"><a href="/wiki/F._A._Forbes" title="F. A. Forbes">F. A. Forbes</a> (1924) [1918]. <i>Life of Pius X</i> (2nd&#160;ed.). New York: PJ Kenedy &amp; Sons.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Life+of+Pius+X&amp;rft.place=New+York&amp;rft.edition=2nd&amp;rft.pub=PJ+Kenedy+%26+Sons&amp;rft.date=1924&amp;rft.au=F.+A.+Forbes&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Pius+X" class="Z3988"></span> Merry del Val (above) considered this work to be the most authoritative written on him.</li>
<li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFRené_Bazin1928" class="citation book cs1"><a href="/wiki/Ren%C3%A9_Bazin" title="René Bazin">René Bazin</a> (1928). <i>Pius X</i>. St Louis: B Herder.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Pius+X&amp;rft.place=St+Louis&amp;rft.pub=B+Herder&amp;rft.date=1928&amp;rft.au=Ren%C3%A9+Bazin&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Pius+X" class="Z3988"></span></li>
<li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFBurton,_Katherine1950" class="citation book cs1"><a href="/wiki/Katherine_Burton" title="Katherine Burton">Burton, Katherine</a> (1950). <span class="cs1-lock-registration" title="Free registration required"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/greatmantlelifeo00burt"><i>The Great Mantle: The Life of Giuseppe Sarto</i></a></span>. Longmens.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=The+Great+Mantle%3A+The+Life+of+Giuseppe+Sarto&amp;rft.pub=Longmens&amp;rft.date=1950&amp;rft.au=Burton%2C+Katherine&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Farchive.org%2Fdetails%2Fgreatmantlelifeo00burt&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Pius+X" class="Z3988"></span></li>
